# 81: Cynthia’s What? (BONUS 2)

In a remote part of Rustboro City's outskirts in Hoenn:

"Ka-kah!" Haunter suddenly shouted, pointing upward.

Another flying one!

A powerful Garchomp glided through the air, landing with a thud that sent dust swirling around.

"Oowu!" Vulpix, alarmed, burrowed its head into Johan's arm, leaving its fluffy tail sticking up. Just from its aura alone, the sight of Garchomp—one of the strongest pseudo-legendary Pokémon in its final form—was enough to intimidate it.

Plus, Garchomp didn't exactly look friendly.

"It's here for you," Johan teased Haunter with a sidelong glance.

"Gulp—"

Haunter swallowed nervously.

"Ka-kah."

Don't say that...

Haunter's face turned a paler shade, though it was hard to tell whether it was out of fear or nerves. Seeing Haunter's unease made Johan relax slightly. A smile tugged at his lips.

Turns out, happiness is constant—it just shifts from the Pokémon to the Trainer.

Tap, tap—

Footsteps echoed.

As the dust settled, a woman appeared with cascading golden hair, dressed in a sleek black coat and fitted trousers. She walked gracefully in elegant black heels, a warm and polite smile on her face.

Future Champion of the Sinnoh region.

Cynthia.

Cynthia's eyes brightened when she saw Johan and Haunter. She walked over, Garchomp following closely behind.

"Garchomp," it growled lowly, never straying far from her side.

With Cynthia's poised presence juxtaposed against Garchomp's fierce visage, the pair embodied a striking "beauty and the beast" contrast.

Cynthia stopped in front of Johan, her gaze meeting his.

"[Grain in the Field]?"

Johan nodded and introduced himself, "Johan."

Recognition lit up Cynthia's expression as she mirrored the introduction, "Cynthia."

Johan chuckled.

"I never imagined 'Eevee Eats Cabbage' would turn out to be you."

"Hmm?" Cynthia tilted her head slightly.

"You're even more beautiful in person than in the videos," Johan added sincerely.

Cynthia's eyes widened slightly at the compliment.

"Thank you."

Her smile deepened as she tucked a stray lock of hair behind her ear.

"You're more eloquent than I expected."

Cynthia's gentle voice seemed to soothe even the timid Vulpix, who peeked out from Johan's arm.

"Oowu~~"

Such a beautiful lady!

Vulpix's eyes sparkled with admiration.

Though she couldn't understand Vulpix's words, Cynthia sensed the sentiment and her eyes crinkled into crescents.

She leaned down toward Vulpix. "Is this the little one?"

She had listened in when Johan and Haunter had saved Vulpix and even offered help back then.

"Yes," Johan replied, lowering his head too.

Up close, Cynthia's flawless features, high-bridged nose, and lightly tinted lips were captivating. A subtle, sweet scent reminiscent of vanilla wafted through the air.

Admittedly, it was quite pleasant.

"Ka-kah!!"

Haunter, who had been stunned for a while, suddenly snapped back, shouting in realization.

I remember who she is!

"Garchomp—"

Garchomp narrowed its eyes and swung its blade-like fin, creating a gust as it positioned itself protectively between Cynthia and Haunter.

The menacing aura made Johan and Haunter freeze, cold sweat trickling down their faces.

"Ka-ka…"

"Oowu!"

Vulpix squeaked and buried itself even deeper.

"Garchomp." Cynthia's brows furrowed as she called its name.

With a grunt, Garchomp lowered its fin, though it kept its sharp eyes locked on Haunter and Johan.

"My apologies," Cynthia said.

"Ka-kah~~" Haunter patted its chest, regaining some composure.

Scared the ghost out of me!

Johan shook his head, exhaling. Though startled by Garchomp's sudden action, he managed to keep calm. After all, his experience included dealing with Mewtwo as a "patient" and having encountered Heatran. Pseudo-legendary Pokémon were nothing new.

"Shall we take a look at Spiritomb?" Johan suggested.

"Of course."

Cynthia didn't hesitate. She tossed a Poké Ball, and with a flash of light, a peculiar keystone appeared on the ground.

From its crack, a swirl of purple and green mist emerged, coalescing into a vaguely face-like form.

Spiritomb!

"Shhhh-pah?"

Spiritomb surveyed the unfamiliar surroundings with wary eyes.

"Ka-kah (・・)"

Seeing Spiritomb, Haunter went stiff.

It actually came…

Johan rubbed his chin, eyes fixed on Spiritomb as he mused, "Honestly, it's got an oddly charming look, don't you think, Haunter?"

"Shhh-pah…o(////▽////)q"

Before Haunter could react, Spiritomb's shadowy face flushed with a tinge of embarrassment.

It was the first time anyone had called it attractive.

Haunter, meanwhile, shot Johan a look that said, Are you insane?

Cynthia smiled warmly. "I think so too."

She glanced at Johan interacting with Spiritomb.

At least their attitudes toward Pokémon were aligned.

Kneeling, Johan spoke softly, "Can you use Nasty Plot?"

"Shhh-pah?"

Spiritomb hesitated, then turned to Cynthia and Garchomp for confirmation.

Cynthia nodded.

"Shhh-pah—"

Spiritomb's aura shifted, emitting a cunning, deceptive energy.

In Johan's vision, Spiritomb's stats appeared:

[Spiritomb]

Type: Ghost/Dark

Ability: Pressure

Moves Known: Night Shade, Confuse Ray, Shadow Sneak, Grudge, Payback, Nasty Plot, Hex, Parting Shot, Sucker Punch, Curse, Shadow Ball, Dark Pulse, Hypnosis, Destiny Bond, Pain Split.

Mood: Happy, Shy, Anxious, Apathetic, Greedy, Angry... (A maze of emotions. But it's currently happy.)

Status: Optimal (Recovered well.)

Friendliness: 50 (You're the first to call it attractive.)

Johan clicked his tongue in surprise.

These are all the moves Haunter dreams of learning.

When he saw Spiritomb's complex array of emotions, Johan's eye twitched.

Wow. That's some real personality fragmentation.

Yet he felt a glimmer of relief seeing Spiritomb's high friendliness towards him.

No matter how convoluted its emotions were, it was still just a Pokémon, one easily pleased by its nature.

Holding up a finger, Johan said gently, "Follow my finger with your eyes. Let's do some simple tests."

"Shhh-pah."

The misty face contracted slightly, nodding obediently.

He's so kind…

Meanwhile, Haunter's eyes rolled mischievously, emitting a sinister chuckle.

Careful under Garchomp's watchful eye, Haunter sidled up to Cynthia.

"Something wrong?" she asked.

She had noticed Haunter's earlier expression, as if it had something to say.

"Ka-kah!"

I remembered who you are!

Haunter had been puzzled because Cynthia looked familiar but couldn't recall from where. Now it clicked!

Haunter opened its mouth, tongue curling out to reveal a phone, which it had stealthily taken from Johan.

In Cynthia's intrigued gaze, Haunter deftly unlocked it and browsed the history.

"Ka-kah!"

Found it!

Haunter grinned smugly.

Cynthia leaned in and murmured, "Cynthia's…"

"Haunter," Johan's voice called.

Haunter jumped in fright, hastily retracting the phone.

Cynthia only glimpsed part of it.

She tilted her head.

Cynthia's what?

Isn't that just me?

The screen seemed to show something about a show?

# 82: Impressive! (BONUS 3)

"Ka-kah," Haunter sheepishly hovered toward Johan.

"What are you two talking about?" Johan asked softly as he guided Spiritomb's eyes with the slow movement of his finger.

"Ka-ka-kah," Haunter stammered, feigning innocence with wide, upward-glancing eyes and a pursed mouth—a clear "I don't know what you're talking about" face. Had Johan not been focused on Spiritomb, he would have seen through Haunter's act in an instant.

"Come here and help me," Johan instructed.

Relieved that Johan wasn't pressing for details, Haunter sighed and hurried over, only to pause midway with sudden realization.

Wait... Spiritomb?

Johan reached out, pulling Haunter to his side firmly. "I was joking earlier. This time, let's be serious."

Haunter, knowing Johan well, understood that when he spoke in this tone, he meant business. Haunter quickly straightened up, thoughts of mischief vanishing.

"Ka-kah?" What do you need me to do?

"Watch Spiritomb closely."

Haunter's gaze followed Johan's guiding finger. In the fog-like aura exuding from the fissures of the Odd Keystone, green spheres of various sizes began to emerge. Most of them moved along with Johan's finger, but a few stayed put—or worse, moved erratically as if trying to sabotage the process.

"Ka-kah..." Haunter whispered, beginning to comprehend Johan's plan.

Spiritomb, a Pokémon made up of 108 different souls, naturally had a mixture of personalities. Among them were benevolent spirits but also malevolent ones, forced into coexistence by the Keystone's seal. Johan's method involved using Spiritomb's trust in him, combined with psychological guidance and subtle hand movements, to identify the uncooperative spirits.

Haunter's role was now clear: to hypnotize those rogue souls.

Completely curing the "fractured personality" of a Spiritomb was virtually impossible unless each soul was removed, but that would destroy its essence. However, turning the disruptive souls neutral or even friendly? That could be done.

In Johan's signal, Haunter's eyes glimmered with a soft red light.

Meanwhile:

"You're doing quite well, Johan must treat you with great care," Cynthia murmured, crouching and gently scratching under Vulpix's chin. The small Pokémon closed its eyes in contentment, purring softly.

"Oowu~~"

Vulpix rarely showed affection toward strangers but made an exception for Cynthia.

"So cute," Cynthia's eyes curved into a smile. "Can I hold you?"

"Oowu..." Vulpix hesitated, glancing at Johan for reassurance.

"Don't feel pressured..."

Before she could finish, Vulpix hopped into her arms with a chirp.

"Oowu~~"

A soft, bell-like laugh escaped Cynthia. Johan's Pokémon had such kind temperaments, she mused. Unlike her own, who often put on a stoic act in public.

She glanced at Garchomp, which stood alert behind her.

"Garchomp?" It tilted its head, puzzled by the attention.

"Oowu~~" Vulpix exhaled a tiny frosty puff, tilting its head as if recalling Johan's comment: Isn't she our 'golden benefactor'?

Cynthia's heart melted, and she nuzzled Vulpix's face. It was soft, cool, and immensely comforting.

Suddenly, Spiritomb's energy quivered, and a sharp green wisp shot out, aiming straight for Johan!

"Haunter!" Johan shouted.

"Ka-kah!"

Haunter didn't need the command. Already prepared, it threw a ball of blue-purple ghostly fire that intercepted the green wisp with a crack.

Spiritomb's souls had the ability to detach and possess others, manipulating their minds. Johan had warned Haunter of this in advance, so it reacted swiftly.

The ghostly flames pushed the green wisp back, which then fell to the ground. A sinister aura spread as it inflated, transforming into a shadowy doppelgänger of Spiritomb. Its eyes glinted with malice.

Haunter blocked its path, eyes narrowing.

"Hypnosis."

"Ka-kah~~" Haunter smirked, eyes glowing red as it locked gazes with the shadowy entity. It froze, then shrank back into a harmless green sphere.

Haunter glanced at Johan, who nodded approval. It nudged the sphere back into Spiritomb's body.

"Ka-kah?" Is it over?

"Not quite," Johan muttered. He noticed the rest of the spheres inside Spiritomb had started to shift and murmur. He lowered his hand to prevent over-stimulation.

Progress was incremental. If pressured too much, the rebellious souls might revolt all at once, causing chaos. But with the most aggressive soul temporarily pacified, Spiritomb's aura grew noticeably calmer.

A step in the right direction.

"Shhhh-pah?" Spiritomb's voice wavered with confusion. Did I do something bad again?

Johan smiled reassuringly. "No, you did great."

"Shhhh-pah? o(////▽////)q" Its foggy form blushed, pleased by the praise.

Cynthia approached, holding Vulpix and with Garchomp by her side, an inquisitive look in her eyes.

Johan shook his head but smiled, saying, "Spiritomb was very cooperative. It behaved well, but this will take time."

Cynthia nodded in understanding. The process was possible but not immediate.

Garchomp found Spiritomb's current docility peculiar. Cynthia recalled it into its Poké Ball, and they walked toward the road.

Cynthia had considered flying Johan to Rustboro City, but one look at Garchomp's stern gaze and Johan preferred the comfort of a taxi.

As they were on the outskirts, finding a cab wasn't easy. Johan called the same taxi driver, the one who'd fainted after encountering Haunter.

Before long, the cab pulled up. The driver rolled down the window, eyes widening at Cynthia. He shot Johan an impressed glance, whispering, "Is she your girlfriend? Impressive, kid."

But his words cut off when he felt Garchomp's glare. Both the driver and Johan stiffened.

"Haha… So, where to?" the driver said, forcing a smile.

Johan glanced at Cynthia.

"The Rustboro Grand Hotel," she said with a serene smile.

The driver's eyes bulged. He turned back to Johan and gave a subtle thumbs-up.

Impressive!

---

---

---

# 83: It’s Serious! (BONUS 4)

Rustboro Grand Hotel was the largest hotel in Rustboro City, owned, of course, by the infrastructure powerhouse, Devon Corporation.

Screech—

The taxi came to a smooth stop in front of the hotel, and attendants quickly stepped forward to welcome the guests.

"Hey, young man," the driver called out as Johan was getting out of the car.

"Hm?" Johan turned back.

The driver wore a melancholic expression, as though reliving countless memories. "If you care about someone, don't wait too long to tell them. You don't want to regret it once it's too late."

Judging by his wistful look, it was clear he had many stories of his own.

Sir, aren't you getting a bit too invested?

"Driver, I—"

Before Johan could respond, the driver tapped the car door and cast aside his nostalgic mood. "That's all!" He started to roll up the window but hesitated, leaning closer and whispering conspiratorially, "This girl looks well-off. You should take the chance to—uh, you know—start a family. Trust me, you—"

Johan's face twitched. "Haunter, would you kindly see the driver off?"

"Ka-kah~~" Haunter grinned mischievously, prompting the driver to go pale.

"No need, thanks, kid!" The driver sped off in a flash.

"What's that about?" Cynthia approached, having just completed her check-in.

"His wife was calling him for dinner."

"He seemed to linger quite a while for someone in a hurry."

"His wife is as fierce as an Incineroar."

"Oh, I see…"

"Would you like to come in?" Cynthia asked as they reached her room's door.

"Not sure if that's appropriate…"

"Ka-kah!" Haunter peeked through the door and beckoned them with a wave. It's huge in here!

Johan sighed, noticing the mischief in Haunter's expression.

Cynthia chuckled softly. "I have a few things to give you."

She opened the door, and Johan followed her inside, unable to help but marvel. No wonder Haunter was excited—the suite was impressively grand. The ceilings were nearly four meters high, and it had a bedroom, living room, kitchen, exercise room, and even a small training arena—clearly tailored for Trainers.

"Having money… is really something."

"Everyone, come out."

As Johan marveled, Cynthia released multiple Poké Balls simultaneously. The room buzzed with activity as beams of red light filled the space.

The fierce-eyed Garchomp stood tall, exuding an intimidating presence. Lucario leaned against the window with crossed arms, looking effortlessly cool. Beside them were Roserade, the recently evolved and graceful Milotic who slid into the outdoor pool, the curious little Eevee, and, finally, Spiritomb.

"Ka-kah! Σ(дlll)" Haunter, who had been floating carelessly, froze in terror, beads of sweat forming at the sight of the powerful Pokémon, especially Garchomp and the ever-stoic Lucario.

A glint from their eyes was enough to make Haunter's face go pale, its floating form stiffening.

"Ka-kah…" Sirs, I was just going to show you a magic trick…

Haunter hesitated, then opened its palms, and a Hypnosis ring turned into sparkling Energy Cubes.

"Garchomp?"

"Lucario?"

The two Pokémon exchanged surprised glances, intrigued.

"Ka-kah?" Want some?

They nodded, their interest piqued.

"Oowu?"

"Eevee?"

Vulpix and Cynthia's Eevee exchanged curious looks. For Eevee, this was a rare opportunity; it was used to being the smallest and facing much larger Pokémon like Garchomp and Lucario. So, seeing another small and adorable Pokémon made it feel a kinship.

With a gentle hop, Eevee bounded over to Vulpix.

"Oowu?" Vulpix instinctively stepped back.

Eevee tilted its head, smiled warmly, and made a heart shape with its ears and paws.

"Oowu!" Vulpix chirped back, intrigued by the gesture.

Meanwhile, Johan approached the pool, crouching down as Milotic surfaced with a splash. Its eyes brightened as it recognized Johan's voice, the one that had soothed it to sleep so many times.

"Seems it likes you a lot," Cynthia observed, joining Johan and stroking Milotic's smooth scales. "Thanks to you, it was able to evolve perfectly."

"It was its own efforts in the end," Johan replied with a modest smile.

"Hmm," Milotic purred softly, nudging him affectionately. Tell me a story?

"What did it say?" Johan asked.

Cynthia's smile widened. "It said it misses your bedtime stories."

Johan glanced between Milotic and Cynthia, whose expression resembled that of a living doll.

Wait, are you sure it was Milotic that said that?

"By the way," Cynthia started, catching his attention.

"Yes?"

"Earlier…" Cynthia's eyes drifted toward Haunter, who was now surprisingly surrounded by Garchomp and Lucario, engaged in sharing Energy Cubes. It was unusual; even Cynthia hadn't seen her Pokémon warm up so quickly to another, especially not with newcomers.

But it looked like Haunter's treats had charmed them.

"Energy Cubes?" she noted.

Johan chuckled. "Things always taste better when they're someone else's, don't they?"

"True…" Cynthia nodded thoughtfully. Even she found that treats at Professor Rowan's home seemed tastier than her grandmother's.

"You were saying?" Johan redirected the conversation.

"Oh, right. Earlier, Haunter showed me something on your phone…" Cynthia trailed off.

What? My phone?! Johan felt a chill run down his spine.

"I think it said something about a… 'Cynthia Show'?" Cynthia tilted her head, looking genuinely curious.

Drawing on all his psychological training, Johan kept his composure, fabricating a calm reply. "Ah, that must've been Haunter's own phone. It has one that looks like mine."

"Hm?"

"It's a fan of battle shows and streams. It must've been watching your battles and was probably looking to get an autograph."

"Oh…" Cynthia's brows furrowed slightly in thought, but she nodded, buying the explanation.

"Shall we check on Spiritomb?" Johan suggested, subtly wiping sweat from his brow as he changed the topic.

Cynthia's expression grew serious. "Is it bad?"

Johan nodded, face turning grave. "Very serious."

---

---

---

# 84: What a Coincidence (BONUS 5)

"Really?" Cynthia's expression turned serious.

Having seen Johan's capabilities firsthand, Cynthia trusted his judgment implicitly.

Johan breathed a quiet sigh of relief. Thankfully, he had successfully diverted the conversation.

He began explaining Spiritomb's complex nature. As a Pokémon composed of 108 different spirits, its unusual behavior was often out of its own control. Spiritomb was essentially a collection of fragmented, sometimes contradictory, consciousnesses. While not all 108 spirits were distinct—some had merged—this still made for a deeply complex mindscape.

Worse, if this delicate balance were disturbed, the malicious, deceitful spirits could break free on their own.

In the original lore, even elite trainers fell victim to Spiritomb's influence, becoming its unwilling puppets.

Surprisingly, Spiritomb's current state was relatively stable, which Johan found unusual—until he remembered that Cynthia was its Trainer. In that case, it made perfect sense.

"I understand." Cynthia took a breath, nodding seriously before walking into the suite's bedroom.

Understand what? Johan thought, puzzled.

Just then, his phone pinged with a new message.

[Your account has been credited: 1,000,000 Pokédollars. Balance: 2,476,723 Pokédollars.]

Johan's eyes widened in disbelief.

A moment later, Cynthia emerged from the room, holding the latest Eevee-themed phone, murmuring to herself, "I didn't realize my allowance was getting so low."

Allowance? Johan was stunned. You call a million Pokédollars an allowance?

Before he could respond, she added, "I know it might not be enough, so after the exhibition match, I'll top it off."

Is this what life with a wealthy benefactor feels like?

Johan's thoughts raced: If only I could have ten more clients like her!

"There is actually a way to expedite Spiritomb's treatment," Johan said as he and Cynthia sat down at the hotel desk to strategize.

"Oh?"

"The best solution is to gather a group of strong Trainers. When the restless spirits within Spiritomb can no longer be contained and start scattering, we'll subdue them all at once." Johan clenched a fist in demonstration.

Cynthia's brows furrowed as she glanced around the room.

Garchomp and Haunter were fixated on a video playing on a phone. Lucario stood nearby with crossed arms, pretending not to care but casting furtive glances at the screen.

Eevee and Vulpix were building a snowman, with Eevee gleefully burying itself in the snow Vulpix created. Milotic was gracefully swimming in the pool while Roserade sat by the edge, feet soaking in the water as they shared grooming tips.

As for Spiritomb… it was nestled in a corner, pretending to be a potted plant.

"Isn't our team enough?" Cynthia asked.

Johan's eyes scanned the room again before he pursed his lips. "Just to be safe."

He added, "Don't worry, I know who to call."

Steven was the first who came to mind. Lance, who would be in Hoenn for the exhibition, was also an obvious choice.

Cynthia, too, considered her contacts: her friend Diantha and Caitlin were likely candidates.

They agreed to maintain Spiritomb's stability until they gathered enough capable Trainers in Sootopolis City.

"Here," Cynthia said, handing Johan two envelopes.

He opened the first one—it was another invitation. Great, he thought wryly. That makes three now. I could probably trade these in for a luxury suite.

The second envelope, however, was unexpected. It was an invitation to the "Pokémon Researcher Conference – Spear Pillar Edition."

"Professor Rowan asked me to give this to you," Cynthia explained. "If you have the time, you should attend."

"I definitely will," Johan replied.

Outside the Rustboro Grand Hotel

"Ka-kah…" Haunter's eyes darted nervously at Johan, who was holding its phone. The screen displayed a streaming platform with a VIP subscription open.

"Do you understand why this is wrong?" Johan's tone was stern.

Haunter looked sheepishly at Johan, then back at the VIP subscription screen. Finally, it nodded emphatically.

Johan flipped to the annual VIP package on the screen, ready to confirm.

"Ka-kah?" Haunter's eyes widened.

"It was an ad, and I clicked on it accidentally while researching. You believe that, right?" Johan pressed.

Cynthia appearing in a swimsuit contest? Clearly clickbait.

Haunter glanced at Johan, then at the subscription page, and nodded solemnly.

"Ka-kah!" I believe!

Hesitating for even a second would be disrespectful to the VIP subscription.

"Good." Johan clicked confirm, gifting Haunter a year's subscription, costing a few hundred Pokédollars.

He sighed, relieved. Crisis averted.

"Oowu?" Vulpix tilted its head, puzzled by the exchange.

"Eevee!" Cynthia's Eevee's cheerful voice sounded as she emerged from the hotel, holding Eevee.

Cynthia had changed into a light blue top with a white casual jacket, exuding a more youthful, lively air.

"Sorry for the wait," she said, a slight blush on her pale cheeks from hurrying.

"What shall we eat?" Johan asked. As her host, it was only proper to treat her to dinner. Besides, Cynthia had given him all her spare cash, forgetting to save any for herself.

The situation made Johan smile ruefully.

"Hmm…" Cynthia's brow knitted in thought, finger twirling her golden hair. The dilemma was evident.

"Eevee…" The small Pokémon face-palmed, resigned to the upcoming indecision.

Johan, realizing what was happening, chuckled. Right. The future Champion of Sinnoh suffers from chronic indecisiveness.

He raised a hand to his temple. "Never mind, let's go with Hoenn's specialty."

Both Cynthia and Eevee visibly relaxed, smiling in relief.

Johan knew Rustboro's dining scene well. If there was one local specialty Sinnoh didn't have, it was "Volcanic Barbecue."

Cooked on unique volcanic stones from Mt. Chimney, the meat carried a distinct, smoky essence. Expensive, but worth it.

Soon, they arrived at "Torkoal Grill," a renowned restaurant in the city center.

At that moment…

"We're late today. We'll visit Johan tomorrow and head to Sootopolis with him and Steven," said a familiar figure with a billowing cape and deep red hair.

Lance, accompanied by the Blackthorn Gym Leader Clair, strolled through Rustboro City.

Hearing Johan's name, Clair's eyes narrowed slightly.

Johan's tactics had made Lance's battles with Lorelei much tougher.

"Since we're off-duty, how about we try this—Hoenn's famous volcanic barbecue?" Lance suggested.

But as they reached the restaurant, they found themselves face-to-face with Johan and Cynthia.

"You…?" Lance's expression was stunned.

Johan blinked in surprise.

"What a coincidence."

# 85: I’ll Battle You (BONUS)

The décor of "Torkoal Grill" was infused with fire elements at every turn. The walls were painted in a vibrant red, with areas purposely charred for effect, adorned with Fire-type Pokémon motifs, paintings, and sculptures.

In a private room of the restaurant, Johan, Cynthia, Lance, and Clair sat across from each other. The atmosphere was somewhat awkward, especially for Lance and Clair, who exchanged glances at Cynthia, who sat beside Johan. Dressed casually, her golden hair cascaded down her back as she played with Vulpix and Eevee, eyes smiling.

Is that really Cynthia?

Though from different regions, the standout figures among the younger generation often knew or had crossed paths, sometimes even battled. Cynthia's prowess was well-recognized, and she had a considerable following online. But Lance and Clair had never heard of her dining alone with a male companion.

What exactly is her connection with Johan?

"Lance, aren't you going to introduce your companion?" Johan interjected, breaking the silence with a grin.

He could tell from their looks that they were drawing conclusions, so he decided to get ahead of it by inquiring about Clair's identity.

Lance blinked, catching on quickly. "This is Clair, my cousin and the current Gym Leader of Blackthorn City."

"Ah, the other Dragon-type Trainer of the Dragon Clan," Johan noted with recognition.

Clair scrunched her nose slightly, giving a short "Mm." Her attitude toward Johan was evidently less than warm.

Johan could guess why—Clair had long admired Lance. And here he was, unintentionally crashing their evening.

Lance turned to them, eyes darting between Johan and Cynthia. "And you two?"

Cynthia's reputation preceded her; seeing her and Johan together puzzled Lance.

Johan chuckled. "You know Cynthia, of course. Like you, she's here as a 'trainer family member.'"

Trainer family member? Lance's eyes widened in realization. "Ah, I see."

"Shall we start grilling, then? I think Haunter can barely wait," he suggested, noting the drooling Ghost-type who was pretending to cry for food.

"Ka-kah~~" Haunter's eyes gleamed as it slurped back its tongue. Finally, someone noticed me! I'm starving over here!

With sleeves rolled up, Johan and Lance began placing slices of fresh meat on the volcanic stone grill.

I see, that's it? Clair thought, frustration bubbling as she listened to Lance's easy conclusion. All that talk, just for a Haunter?

She sighed internally at her cousin's cluelessness.

The hiss and sizzle of meat on the stone filled the air as the rich scent of roasting meat spread. Soon, Johan and Lance piled a plate high with grilled pieces, setting it down before going for the next round.

"Ka-kah~~" Haunter seized the moment, grabbing tongs and distributing meat to Cynthia, Clair, Vulpix, and Eevee. It then deftly wrapped its tongue around the remaining slices and swallowed them in one go.

"Ka-kah! (O_O;)"

"…Isn't that too hot?" Johan blinked, half amused and half exasperated. Anyone who didn't know better would think he mistreated Haunter.

"Ka-kah(;Д｀)" Haunter opened its mouth, revealing its now redder-than-usual tongue, blistered with tiny bubbles.

Johan sighed, handing over a healing spray. "Silly thing."

"Oowu…" Vulpix murmured, drawing Haunter's attention. The icy fox breathed a gentle gust of chill over the plates in front of them, instantly cooling them.

"Thank you," Cynthia cooed, petting Vulpix on the head.

"Oowu…" Embarrassed, Vulpix lowered its head, tail wagging furiously.

"Ka-kah!! (PД`q.)·.'゜" Haunter's eyes filled with tears. It had forgotten Vulpix had this handy "cooling feature."

As conversation flowed and laughter eased the group's tension, they became more comfortable around each other.

"Any plans after dinner?" Lance asked suddenly.

The question triggered Cynthia's decision-making paralysis. Johan knew trouble was brewing. Before he could blurt out "going home to sleep," Lance beat him to it.

"How about a battle?"

In this world, a battle invitation between Trainers was like saying, "Want to play some ball?"—irresistibly enticing.

But Johan had no interest in sparring with the likes of these three. Lance and Cynthia were future Champions of their regions, and even Clair was strong enough to contend with the Elite Four one day.

Johan, a psychology expert, battling them? Even with ten Haunters, he'd still stand no chance.

Clair's initial disappointment at Lance's suggestion of battling—she had hoped for a movie night featuring Diantha—quickly shifted to a burning passion. Lance was a battle enthusiast, and Clair was no different.

She glanced at Johan, a challenge sparking in her eyes. "I'd like to see some of Johan-sensei's tactics."

Johan's battle strategies had gained a modest following online. Besides, in Lance's fight against Lorelei, she'd noticed traces of Johan's strategic influence.

"Not really necessary," Johan said, smiling wryly. "I'm just a Pokémon psychology teacher, not skilled in battle. Plus, in terms of strength…" He looked at Haunter, who was busy inhaling more food, and Vulpix, chilling a drink with icy breath.

They hadn't grasped the "seriousness" of the situation at all.

"I could use my second team," Clair proposed quickly.

"Or…" Johan started, but Clair cut him off.

"If you beat me, this 'Dragon Fang' is yours." She pulled out a sharp, glistening fang that radiated draconic energy.

Lance massaged his temples. He knew his cousin's stubbornness well. As one of the top talents in their family, Clair was notoriously headstrong. Her whip wasn't just for training dragons; sometimes, it found its way onto human shoulders.

Cynthia, who had been playing with Vulpix and Eevee, frowned slightly and held up a hand. "I'll battle you."

Lance and Clair looked at Cynthia in surprise.

Johan rubbed his temples. Might as well…

"Alright, I'll do it."

# 86: My Dearest Companion (BONUS)

Within the closed battle arena in Rustboro City:

"Gyarados, Hydro Cannon!"

With a sweeping motion, Lance's cape flared dramatically as Gyarados's power surged.

"Rooaaar—!"

The roar was accompanied by a torrent of water erupting from Gyarados's mouth, crashing forth with immense force.

"Kingdra, dodge quickly!"

Across the field, Clair's expression was tense as she lashed her whip, signaling Kingdra to evade. But Lance and Gyarados had already anticipated Kingdra's movements. The Hydro Cannon hit its mark perfectly.

Boom!

Water splashed and sprayed, creating a prism of colors under the bright arena lights.

Watching from the stands, Johan winced slightly.

Hitting such a fast-moving Kingdra with a low-accuracy move like Hydro Cannon... this is what battling with a prodigy looks like?

Memories of his own five consecutive misses with Hydro Cannon came to mind.

Prodigies... so unfair.

Cynthia, seated beside him, shared a similar look of contemplation. Lance's skill was undeniable.

"Isn't it a bit much just for a warm-up?" Johan muttered.

Clair, although frustrated, kept her composure. Losing to Lance was nothing new. Still, Johan couldn't help but think, Lance's emotional intelligence rivals Red's... just marry her already.

After the battle, Lance walked over, smiling as he "consoled" (mocked) Clair. Her flushed face grew redder as she shot a glare at Johan.

Noticing Clair's gaze, Cynthia softly asked, "Should I take this battle instead?"

Johan's lips twitched as he shook his head. "No need. Winning or losing isn't the point."

Besides, he thought, if Cynthia goes up and wins, Clair might throw her Pokéball... the wrong way. That would be a disaster.

Lance approached, calling out, "Johan, it's your turn. Be careful, though—Clair's in a bad mood today. Even her second team is strong."

Johan stood up, casting Lance a side glance. You really don't know why she's upset, do you?

"Oowu?"

Vulpix looked up, puzzled, as Johan scooped it up from Cynthia's lap.

Clair, despite her irritation, was strategic and composed. She released her next Pokémon: a Dragonair.

Dragonair, an elegant pure Dragon-type, evolved from Dratini and could eventually become the formidable Dragonite, the pseudo-legendary of Kanto.

Johan couldn't help but recall his early confusion when first encountering Pokémon games. How does such a graceful Pokémon evolve into... Dragonite? It makes no sense.

Still, Dragonair, as a second-stage Dragon-type, commanded respect. Especially under Clair's expert guidance.

Setting Vulpix down, Johan whispered words of encouragement.

"Oowu..."

Realizing it was going into battle, Vulpix's eyes darted to Dragonair, lighting up with excitement.

So beautiful!

Clair, meanwhile, frowned. Vulpix seemed more like a dainty pet than a serious fighter.

It can't be underestimated, Johan thought, glancing at his team. Most Trainers struggled to maintain a single strong team, let alone nurture a second one filled with pseudo-legendaries like Clair and Lance had.

However, the outcome of a battle wasn't solely determined by the power of the Pokémon. Strategy, training, and the bond between Trainer and Pokémon mattered just as much.

Vulpix was his choice partly due to its Ice-type advantage over Dragons. Additionally, Dragonair's likely Shed Skin ability would weaken the impact of Haunter's usual status-inflicting moves.

[Shed Skin: The Pokémon may heal its own status conditions periodically.]

From the sidelines:

"Ka-kah, Ka-kah~~"

Haunter had somehow procured two pom-poms and was shaking them energetically in the stands.

"Haunter," Cynthia called out, arms resting under her chin.

"Ka-kah?"

Haunter froze, shoving the pom-poms into its mouth and floating over to give Cynthia an eager shoulder massage.

Cynthia, smirking, looked pleased and asked, "What was it you wanted to show me earlier?"

Johan had given an explanation, but Cynthia couldn't shake the feeling something was off. She intended to press Haunter for answers.

Haunter's grin faltered, eyes drifting upwards nervously.

"Ka-kah?"

Show what? I have no idea...

Cynthia's eyes narrowed, voice dropping to a whisper. "I heard Garchomp's been wanting to spar with you."

"Ka-kah!! Σ(дlll)" Haunter went pale, even for a Ghost-type.

A sly smile curved on Cynthia's lips as she continued, "Oh, and I happen to have an extra 'Shadow Ball' TM... so, what do you think?"

"Ka-kah..." Haunter's eyes sparkled, drool forming.

Shadow Ball! Johan had been trying to get one for Haunter but either couldn't find it or found it too pricey.

"Ka-kah!" Haunter's face turned serious, nodding fervently.

Johan's my beloved Trainer, my friend, my...

"Really?" Cynthia sighed, pretending to muse. "I was also going to take Garchomp to meet its idol, Bubbles the Marill..."

"Ka-kah?! (﹃)" Haunter squealed, tone leaping several pitches.

Is that true?!

Cynthia's smile deepened.

On the battlefield:

Johan, oblivious to the side drama, focused on Vulpix. "I know you're careful about hurting others, but this time, don't hold back. The opponent is strong."

"Oowu?" Vulpix blinked, looking at Johan for confirmation.

Johan chuckled, scratching under its chin.

"Oowu~~" Vulpix's eyes closed in contentment.

"Give it your all."

"Oowu!" Vulpix's eyes gleamed with resolve, turning to face Clair's Dragonair.

Clair, once dismissive of Vulpix, now studied it more seriously. Dragonair coiled, muscles tensing, and waited for the first move.

As the battle began, gentle snowflakes began to fall, drifting softly across the arena.

# 87: Snowy Hide and Seek?

Battle start!

Clair's cape snapped as she raised her whip with a sharp crack. "Dragonair, use Dragon Breath!"

"Rooar—!!"

Dragonair, slender and majestic, let out an ethereal cry. The blue gem on its forehead shimmered as it unleashed a breath of fiery blue-purple energy toward Vulpix.

At the same time, Johan's Pokédex confirmed what he had anticipated: Dragonair's ability was Shed Skin.

"Keep moving, Icy Wind," Johan instructed calmly.

With his composure, Vulpix mirrored his confidence, darting through the swirling snow on its short legs. Despite its stature, Vulpix moved with surprising agility. Enhanced by the Snow Warning ability, its Icy Wind seemed stronger than usual.

Boom!

The fiery breath clashed with the frosty wind, erupting into a cloud of steam.

"Thunder Wave," Clair commanded.

It was clear Clair had predicted Johan and Vulpix's strategy, even anticipating their dodging pattern. As the steam dissipated, sparks of golden electricity danced from Dragonair's gem.

"Rooar..."

Dragonair's breath showed its discomfort, the falling snow and dipping temperature unnerving it. As a Dragon-type, it had an instinctive aversion to cold. While Dragonair could change the weather under normal circumstances, doing so in battle required concentration and energy.

"It's rare to see someone predict Johan's moves," Lance murmured from the sidelines.

Cynthia, her cheeks faintly flushed, propped her chin on her hand. "The Snow Warning needs to play its role to create an advantage..."

Lance glanced sideways, noting Haunter's innocent look and Cynthia's slight pout.

What's going on here?

His thoughts shifted back to the battle as snow thickened, reducing visibility. Multiple images of Vulpix appeared in the snow, much to Lance and Cynthia's surprise.

"Could it be... Snow Cloak?"

But Vulpix's ability was Snow Warning, making it impossible to have Snow Cloak as well. A Pokémon couldn't have two abilities.

[Snow Cloak: Boosts evasion in snowy weather.]

Cynthia squinted, realization dawning. "It looks like... Substitute?"

Substitute?

Lance blinked, watching the multiplying Vulpix figures. Could Vulpix really make so many substitutes?

Then he pieced it together.

The snow?

It was using the snow to build decoys. Lance and Cynthia exchanged glances, trying to fathom Johan's strategy.

Is this... a game of snowy hide and seek?

Zzzt!

Golden arcs from Thunder Wave struck one of the decoys, scattering harmlessly.

Johan's lips curled up slightly. "Vulpix, Nasty Plot."

Vulpix stopped exhaling frost and its eyes darkened with a calculating gleam.

Building snow figures was something Vulpix often did for fun. Johan never discouraged it; instead, he incorporated it into training and tactics.

"Dragonair, use Twister!" Clair ordered.

"Rooar—"

The gem on Dragonair's tail glowed as it twirled, summoning a swirling tornado. Snow was lifted and churned, clearing the field and revealing Vulpix's silhouette.

The move seemed to push back the snowstorm, but in truth, the tide had already turned.

Weather teams thrived on seizing the initiative. Although Vulpix's Snow Warning only affected itself, minimizing its impact, the advantage was now Johan's.

"Vulpix, help it out—Aurora Beam."

"Oowu—"

Vulpix opened its mouth, releasing a silvery beam of light.

Crack, crack, crack—

The icy Twister solidified, its frigidness forcing Dragonair to shudder from the cold.

"Dragon Dance and ascend!" Clair shouted.

"Rooar!"

Dragonair's body glowed as its inner power surged, its temperature rising while it performed a graceful, powerful dance, then lifted into the air.

Taking flight? Johan thought, narrowing his eyes.

Even a novice could see what Clair intended.

"Vulpix, Baby-Doll Eyes."

"Oowu~~"

Vulpix's whine was soft and pitiful, drawing Dragonair's attention momentarily. Its wide eyes blinked, evoking a brief hesitation in the graceful serpent.

"Dragonair!" Clair called out, snapping it back to focus.

"Dragon Rush!"

A bright blue aura enveloped Dragonair, taking on the shape of a fierce dragon as it charged downward. The sheer power cleaved through the snow, aimed squarely at Vulpix.

The pressure was palpable.

Johan's eyes tightened as he watched the attack. Clair's strategy mirrored Lance's—overwhelming force to crush any defense.

Yet the synergy between Clair and her Dragonair fell just short compared to Johan and Vulpix.

"Jump!"

"Oowu!"

Without needing to say more, Vulpix's body shimmered as it leapt back, leaving an exact likeness behind.

Substitute!

Boom!

Dragonair's attack struck like a comet, shattering the substitute as snow sprayed everywhere.

Even in mid-air, Vulpix was jostled by the impact.

So strong... Vulpix's eyes narrowed in focus.

"Aurora Beam," Johan called out.

"Oowu!"

A silvery beam erupted once more, coating Dragonair with frost.

Crack, crack...

Ice formed on its scales.

"Rooar..." Dragonair's voice faltered, cut short as another Aurora Beam followed, freezing it completely.

Vulpix unleashed one more blast for good measure, ensuring Dragonair was encased in ice.

"Enough, Vulpix," Johan said, noticing the layer of ice had grown a few meters thick.

Cynthia and Lance exchanged looks of disbelief.

He won?

When had Johan seized control of the match so decisively?

Both recalled the turning point: when Vulpix played its game of snowy hide and seek, shifting control away from Clair.

A single Pokémon wielding both Snow Warning and the benefits of Snow Cloak—how could they counter that?

# 88: Did You... See It?

"Great job."

Johan never held back on praise for Vulpix. Smiling, he scooped it up and popped an energy cube into its mouth.

"Oowu~~ (`)"

Its mouth full, the little creature gave a muffled response.

Whatever else one might say, the teamwork between Johan and Vulpix was significantly stronger than that between Clair and her Dragonair.

Clair recalled Dragonair, frowning as she mulled over the recent match.

In real battles, Pokémon moves weren't just executed like in games. Not only were there multiple options, but all decisions happened in the blink of an eye, requiring trainers to react and issue commands instantly. Unlike in games, there was no time to ponder for even a few seconds.

At that moment, Clair felt that as a trainer, she had been completely outmatched by Johan.

This guy's command skills… they might even be a bit better than her cousin's!

Clair glanced over to Johan, who was playing with Vulpix, both looking as carefree as ever.

Sighing, she realized they'd also lost soundly in terms of rapport with their Pokémon.

"Johan always surprises me with new strategies," Lance marveled from the stands.

If Johan's earlier "Substitute Toxic" and "Perish Song" strategies could be studied and copied, this new strategy was purely tailored to his Pokémon's characteristics.

Suddenly, a thought hit Lance. He quickly reached for his shoulder, where his official "Recording Badge" was mounted.

It was off. He sighed in relief.

Thank goodness… This match hadn't been recorded.

Skarmory's "Substitute Toxic" had been okay. Even "Perish Song" was acceptable. But if this "Snowy Hide-and-Seek" tactic hit the internet and caught the attention of that "old lady" at Silver Academy… things wouldn't end well for him.

Lance didn't know, though, that this strategy was just the beginning of Johan's ideas for Vulpix.

Vulpix still had many moves to learn, like Aurora Veil, Blizzard, Freeze-Dry, and Pain Split, which would add even more layers to their strategy.

And if, one day, Vulpix evolved into an Alolan Ninetales and gained the Fairy type, it would become a true "Dragon Slayer."

And, naturally, a "Flying-type Slayer" too.

"Here, a deal's a deal!" Clair handed over a Dragon Fang to Johan.

The Blackthorn Gym, or rather the entire Dragon Clan of Blackthorn City, had a tradition. Every so often, they held a "Dragon Fang Ceremony" to award dragon trainers.

So while Dragon Fang was a rare treasure for most trainers, it wasn't a huge deal for Clair.

"Well, if you insist…"

Johan smoothly pocketed the Dragon Fang with practiced ease.

Clair pursed her lips. "I'll admit you're an excellent strategist with impressive skills in raising Pokémon, but next time we battle, I won't be using my second team!"

Johan nodded with a grin. He had no intention of there being a "next time."

Clair didn't know what he was thinking, but losing twice in a row—to both Lance and Johan—left her in a foul mood.

After a hasty farewell to Johan and Cynthia, Lance quickly followed Clair out.

With Lance, Clair, and their Pokémon gone, the large battle arena was left to Johan, Cynthia, Haunter, and Vulpix.

But for some reason, Johan suddenly felt an odd tension in the air.

Casually glancing at Cynthia, he found her smiling at him with a curious, almost mischievous glint in her eyes, a light blush dusting her cheeks.

Hmm?

Johan blinked.

Following Cynthia's line of sight, he glanced over at Haunter.

Noticing Johan's gaze, Haunter immediately looked away, whistling innocently.

Meanwhile, a Poké Ball had appeared in Cynthia's hand, though her expression remained the same as she slowly walked closer with that gentle smile.

Johan began to sweat.

Instinct told him to run.

Reason told him that there was no escape.

And keeping silent might be his safest bet.

"Did you… see it?"

As she drew closer, her cheeks flushed, Cynthia asked softly.

See it!

The moment Cynthia spoke, Johan knew exactly what she meant. He'd been ratted out by Haunter once again!

As he noticed the Poké Ball in her hand, his rational mind quickly took over.

Feigning innocence, he looked at her blankly. "See what?"

One perk of studying psychology was a relatively strong mental resilience. Johan held firm, his expression betraying nothing that could give him away.

Cynthia scrutinized him for what felt like a long minute, her cheeks tinged with a warm blush. Eventually, she gave a soft huff, wrinkled her nose, and said, "I'm going to buy ice cream!"

Then she briskly walked out of the battle arena.

Once outside, she immediately pulled out her phone.

"All those ads with… certain images of me—find out which companies are responsible," she ordered.

Meanwhile, back inside the arena.

With Cynthia gone, Johan's face darkened as he advanced on Haunter.

Haunter froze, swallowed, and exclaimed, "Ka-Kah!"

I didn't take your phone!

To demonstrate, Haunter spat out its own phone and swiftly pulled up the very same "Cynthia swimsuit show" ad, showing it to Johan.

Haunter had tactfully used its own phone to show Cynthia the ad and point out how her likeness was being misused. It hadn't directly implicated Johan in any way!

"There's… tact?" Johan's eye twitched.

Sure enough, his phone remained safely in his pocket.

From Cynthia's reaction, Haunter hadn't fully sold him out—only halfway.

"Ka-kah…" Haunter let out a relieved sigh and mumbled to itself.

It's actually kind of fun to be a sneaky ghost.

Seeing this, Johan's brow shot up.

Forget it—I can't take this anymore!

Rolling up his sleeves, he lunged at Haunter.

"Ka-Kah!!"

Don't come any closer!

Vulpix hurriedly covered its eyes, too scared to watch… though it left a small gap to peek through.

"Oowu…"

So brutal...

Two minutes later…

Johan sat on the ground, his face slightly purple.

With a Sitrus Berry in one hand and a Pecha Berry in the other, he took alternating bites.

Haunter hovered behind him, smiling ingratiatingly as it massaged his shoulders.

"Ka-kah?"

Need another punch? I actually don't mind…

Realizing the absurdity of what he'd just tried to do, Johan shook his head in resignation.

What had he been thinking, charging a ghost Pokémon alone? He almost thought he was a Super Trainer.

"Let's put this incident to rest," Johan finally said, sighing.

"Ka-Kah~~"

"Oowu~~"

Johan let out another sigh.

"I swear… I really did just accidentally click it… You believe me, right?"

"Ka-Kah!" (Of course! If I didn't, I'd be a ghost!)

"Oowu~~" (If I didn't, I'd be a fox!)

Johan covered his face.

It seemed both of his "little troublemakers" were turning mischievous. Maybe it was time to train up a third.

# 89: Treading on Thin Ice All Day (BONUS)

For 300 powerstones.

-----------

After (being) dealt with by Johan, Haunter watched him dispel the paralysis and poison effects on himself before following Cynthia outside.

He didn't want to leave her alone to buy ice cream; she might take all night to decide. Besides, she didn't have any money on her, and the last thing he needed was Cynthia showing up tomorrow for "compensation" in the form of a combined assault from Garchomp and Lucario.

Finding Cynthia wasn't hard at all.

Not only could Haunter phase through walls, but Vulpix had already memorized her scent. After rounding two corners, they came upon the busy street.

While Kanaz City's night market couldn't compete with the one in Saffron City, it was definitely something unique. Under neon lights that turned night to day, its winding paths stretched for several kilometers, a testament to Kanaz City's bustling charm.

Sure enough, just ahead at an ice cream cart, Cynthia was bent over, thoughtfully selecting flavors.

It was already fall, and it looked like she was the only one brave enough to still be at the ice cream cart. The vendor's awkward expression made it clear she'd been there for a while.

As Johan drew closer, he overheard her murmuring, "Vanilla with cream… maybe strawberry-mint… and chocolate…"

Of course.

But Cynthia's calm manner reassured him that she had likely let the earlier incident go.

"Ka-kah~~"

Before Johan could say anything, Haunter's eyes lit up at the sight of the ice cream cart, and it immediately plastered itself against it, startling the vendor.

Johan chuckled as he pulled Haunter back and said, "Boss, can we get four strawberry-mint chocolate parfaits?"

"Right away!"

The vendor, relieved, hurried to prepare the order.

Cynthia glanced at Johan.

"Tsk." Johan smacked his lips and looked toward the night sky. "Nice night, huh? The moon's looking round. Don't you think so?"

"Ka-kah?"

Haunter put a hand above its eyes, scanning the sky left and right.

Where was the moon?

Johan's cheek twitched.

Cynthia covered her mouth, laughing softly. She reached over and took Vulpix from Johan's arms.

"Young man, here's your ice cream."

Thankfully, the vendor had saved him from any further awkwardness.

"Thanks." Johan nodded as he took the ice cream and handed one to Cynthia, while Haunter, watching eagerly, also got one, along with Vulpix.

"Thank you." Cynthia took a delicate lick of hers.

Eating ice cream in fall felt a bit chilly, and as soon as she took a bite, her eyes squeezed shut.

Haunter, on the other hand, took a big lick with its oversized tongue.

"Ka-Kah? Σ(дlll)"

Haunter stared, stunned, at the empty wrapper in its hand.

Where did my ice cream go?!

It looked helplessly at Johan.

Johan sighed and went back to buy it another one.

Finally, they strolled into the lively night market together.

"You know," Johan said, "there's really no need to struggle over flavor choices. Just buy everything on the menu."

After all, Garchomp had sent word through Haunter that they might, just might, be able to "cure" Cynthia's extreme indecisiveness.

"Hmm." Cynthia nodded thoughtfully. "That's what I used to do."

"You used to?"

Johan blinked.

"When I couldn't decide on clothes, I'd just buy the whole store. But then I found… taking my time to choose felt better."

The whole store?

"Never mind, then."

Johan knew he couldn't "cure" this condition.

Rich people's troubles… he just couldn't relate. Maybe Steven and Cynthia would get along with each other.

After a while, they bought some snacks and Johan walked Cynthia back to her hotel.

At the entrance, he was about to leave when Cynthia turned to him and asked, "Aren't you coming up?"

Johan glanced at the pitch-black night sky.

"I don't think that'd be… appropriate."

Five minutes later:

"Goodnight."

Click!

Standing in the hallway, Johan held Vulpix in one arm and an "TM Shadow Ball" in his hand.

"Ka-kah~~"

Haunter was grinning mischievously.

Want me to open the door for you?

Johan's brow furrowed.

Would you like Garchomp's scythe to be even sharper?

"All that just for this?"

He waved the TM in his hand.

For this thing, he'd nearly missed out on "Fivefold Farewell."

"Ka-kah!"

Haunter nodded enthusiastically.

It's worth at least hundreds of thousands!

Johan was momentarily speechless. He wasn't sure whether this was good or bad.

Because the TM Shadow Ball did cost that much.

It was just that… the way he'd acquired it…

When he finally left the hotel doors, Johan let out a long sigh.

"I was truly walking on thin ice today…"

Thankfully, the whole incident seemed to be behind them.

What he didn't know was how many small companies using the name "Cynthia" to drive web traffic were about to go bankrupt.

Back home, Johan lay sprawled on the couch, too drained to make energy cubes.

Mainly because he'd planned to restock on berries today, but Cynthia's unexpected arrival had derailed those plans.

"But all in all, not a bad day."

He'd earned a million from Cynthia for "treating" Spiritomb, won a Dragon Fang from Clair, and Haunter got its hands on a "TM Shadow Ball."

Adding it all up, he'd probably raked in over two million today!

"Raising these two troublemakers is no easy task…"

Johan looked over at Haunter and Vulpix.

Haunter was focusing intently on learning the TM for Shadow Ball.

For it, offensive moves were a bit limited. Besides Hex, it didn't have many powerful moves, so it had to rely on chip damage strategies.

But with a Pokémon like Clair's Dragonair, with its Shed Skin ability, Haunter's options were limited.

Once it learned Shadow Ball, it would gain a much-needed boost.

"Ka-kah Ka-kah—"

Haunter looked at the TM intently, nodding as if understanding it more and more.

Occasionally, it even split off a Substitute, seemingly testing something that left Johan with an ominous feeling.

Are you actually planning to use Substitute to execute moves?

The league would definitely ban that tactic, right?

But watching Haunter fail time and again, he could tell this was no easy feat.

Meanwhile…

Vulpix continued its "snowman mission" on the ground.

After the battle with Dragonair, Vulpix seemed even more enthusiastic, having realized that snowmen could be useful in actual combat.

This discovery had reignited its passion for snow sculpting.

And actually, the snowmen weren't just for fun.

Johan had encouraged Vulpix to start sculpting with its psychic powers.

Not only could it create them faster, but it also improved its control over its psychic abilities.

While the two were getting a bit "crooked" in terms of how they trained, Johan couldn't deny their effort, which he found comforting.

After resting a bit, Johan got up to take a shower.

Passing by his cabinet, he noticed something.

Was the Pokémon egg… moving a little?

It dawned on him that…

His third "little one" might be ready to "hatch" any day now.

# 90: The 'Patient' Exhibition Match (BONUS)

Bonus for 400 powerstones.

--------

Nighttime.

The little "family of three" was gathered around—not for shaved ice and a movie this time—but around a Pokémon egg.

In fact, this egg had been with Johan even longer than Vulpix had.

Haunter, somehow having acquired a pair of sunglasses, wore them as it gave Vulpix a sly look.

"Ka-kah~~ (😎)"

"Vulpix, you know… technically, you're actually third in line here."

Vulpix tilted its head.

"Oowu?"

Third? Then who's first?

Haunter gave a toothy grin, thumping its chest to assert its seniority.

Vulpix nodded, seeming to agree.

"Oowu?"

Then… this egg is second?

Haunter wagged a finger.

"Ka-kah~~"

Not quite.

Just as it was about to point at someone else, Johan shot it a warning look, muttering darkly:

"If you point at me, I'll challenge Cynthia's Garchomp tomorrow."

"Ka-Kah!! Σ(дlll)"

Haunter quickly waved its hands.

Perish the thought! A joke like that could send a ghost straight to the afterlife!

Vulpix rolled on the floor, laughing.

Recently, it had become much more playful. Though still shy around strangers, it had grown braver compared to before.

"Oowu!"

Suddenly, Vulpix sat up and perked its ears, as though hearing something.

It cautiously leaned toward the egg and pressed one ear to the shell.

Haunter and Johan also quieted, leaning in to listen.

Tap… tap-tap.

Faintly…

It sounded like something was tapping from inside the egg.

"Ka-kah?" Haunter whispered.

It's about to hatch?

But the tapping soon subsided.

"Ka-kah?"

Did it… suffocate?

Johan stopped Haunter before it could dive inside the egg for a look. He sighed and said, "It's just a bit of movement. It's not ready to hatch yet."

Joseph Stone hadn't specified what kind of Pokémon would emerge, but the egg had been housed in a sophisticated Devon Corporation incubator.

It didn't show the exact time remaining, but the approximate progress bar indicated that it was close—probably within the next few days.

"Alright, let's get some rest. We've got things to do tomorrow."

Johan carefully placed the egg back in its case.

Hopefully, Haunter wouldn't corrupt whatever hatched from it.

The next morning, Johan woke up to a sudden, loud crash.

Vulpix, who had been curled up next to him, leapt into the blankets in fright, pressing its cold nose against him and waking him up fully.

Blinking, Johan looked up to see Haunter sheepishly trying to hide something.

Knowing Haunter well, Johan immediately motioned for it to reveal the damage.

Reluctantly, Haunter floated aside to reveal a large hole in the couch.

"Ka-kah!"

Now, listen…

Before Johan could speak, Haunter hurriedly began to explain.

It raised one claw dramatically, and a dark sphere began to form in its hand.

Shadow Ball?

Johan blinked at the sphere's ominous energy.

Haunter had learned it so quickly?

Vulpix peeked out from under the blankets, watching Haunter's demonstration.

But then…

The initially stable black sphere began to wobble, expand, and shrink unpredictably.

Both Johan and Vulpix's expressions shifted as they realized the implications.

If Shadow Ball detonated in the room, it would be no small matter.

But Haunter reacted fast, shoving the unstable sphere into its mouth with a loud "ka-kah!"

Bam!!

The muffled blast echoed inside Haunter, whose entire body puffed up, leaving another gaping hole on the couch, faintly scorched.

"Ka-kah~~"

Haunter flashed a blackened grin, wisps of dark smoke escaping its mouth.

Johan & Vulpix: "…"

"You… okay?"

Johan asked cautiously.

Ghost-type moves deal double damage to Ghost-types, after all.

Haunter had once again strayed into Johan's knowledge blind spot.

For a seasoned Pokémon enthusiast and psychology expert, Johan sometimes found himself questioning his own expertise.

"Ka-kah Ka-kah~~"

Haunter waved off Johan's concern, insisting it was fine.

Johan shook his head and got up to wash up, warning, "Just… be careful. Don't blow yourself up."

"Ka-kah Ka-kah!"

"Oowu?"

Vulpix tilted its head, sensing something was off.

And then it dawned on it.

Ohhh~~ the couch!

Big Brother Haunter didn't get scolded?

And actually…

As Johan headed to the bathroom, Haunter's eyes filled with exhaustion, as though it could barely breathe, clutching its chest in a dramatic fashion.

Vulpix jumped back in alarm.

Are you… still with us?

But then Vulpix's ears perked again. It could've sworn it heard tapping from the egg again.

The egg seemed almost… happy?

Afternoon.

At Steven's estate.

Johan crouched in front of the now-slumbering Spiritomb.

With the combined efforts of Haunter and Spiritomb's own cooperation, lulling it into hypnosis hadn't taken much effort.

Compared to when he'd first encountered it, Spiritomb's dark green energy orbs were now far dimmer, and its overall energy seemed more lively.

Snap!

Johan snapped his fingers.

"Hiss—hiss-pop."

Spiritomb slowly awoke, and upon seeing Johan's warm smile, it flushed with embarrassment.

"How is it?"

Cynthia approached, her expression one of concern.

Originally, they'd planned to wait until they reached Sootopolis to gather additional help for Spiritomb's case.

But now that Lance was already here, with Clair, Steven, and Cynthia's Pokémon, they had more than enough strength, so they'd arranged to meet at Steven's manor that afternoon.

"It's much better."

Johan patted Spiritomb's vaporous form gently.

"A couple more sessions, and it should be okay."

"Hiss-pop~~o(////▽////)q"

"That's good." Cynthia exhaled in relief, patting her chest.

She'd been quite worried about Spiritomb.

Meanwhile…

Steven, Lance, and Clair gathered around a weathered, ancient stone tablet.

This was the second matter they had to address today.

Johan had asked Cynthia previously to help decipher the tablet's inscriptions. With her own research and assistance from her grandmother, Professor Carolina, she'd managed to decode a bit of it.

If he'd had any doubts before, the appearance of "Ancient Relicanth" in the translation convinced him completely.

This tablet was undoubtedly the "Proclamation Tablet"!

A key to the legend of the three sleeping Regi Titans!

But he kept this to himself.

Partly because there wasn't any hard evidence to back it up yet.

But mostly because Steven and Lance's gleaming eyes were giving him chills.

Waking the Regi Titans prematurely might lead to unpredictable consequences…

Seeing the group's attention shift to discussing potential battles, Johan heaved a silent sigh of relief.

Wait a minute!

A strange look crossed his face.

Lance, Steven, Cynthia…

They were all set to participate in the "Youth Exhibition Match" in Sootopolis.

Why did it suddenly feel like…this might turn into a "Patient Exhibition Match"?

Beep beep—

His phone vibrated.

[Forever Eighteen]: Got time?

# 91: “I Remember Again!” (BONUS)

Bonus for 500 powerstones, once again thank you.

--------

[Grain in the Field]: Uh, sure, what's up?

[Forever Eighteen]: Are you currently in the Hoenn region?

Johan paused, puzzled by the unexpected question.

But since his trainer card clearly marked his location, he saw no harm in answering honestly.

[Grain in the Field]: I am.

[Forever Eighteen]: Great! (sigh of relief.jpg) I'll be in Hoenn soon for a tournament and was wondering… if maybe I could stop by afterward? My Smoochum has been dying to meet your Vulpix.

Hm?

Soon?

A tournament in Hoenn?

Could it be... the Youth Exhibition Match?

[Grain in the Field]: Sure, no problem.

On the other side of the conversation, Lorelei exhaled in relief; this would let her properly thank Johan face-to-face, and she already knew just how.

Putting his phone away, Johan glanced at Vulpix, who was busy building a snowman with Cynthia's Eevee, both of them seemingly having a great time.

So Vulpix might actually get to meet Smoochum after all… though how it might feel about an unexpected new "rival" was anyone's guess.

As for Haunter…

It was nearby, eagerly receiving pointers on Shadow Ball from Lucario.

It seemed to be getting along quite well with Cynthia's team.

Of course, among all the Pokémon here, none could rival the rivalry brewing between Garchomp, Metagross, and Dragonite.

All three pseudo-legendary Pokémon felt the intensity of each other's power.

Especially Lance's Dragonite and Cynthia's Garchomp, while Steven's Metagross took on more of a "mediator" role—though its personality wasn't quite as combative as the other two.

Johan couldn't make out what the three were "discussing," but he could guess…

it probably involved Dragon Dance.

Before long, Steven and Lance finally lost interest in the "Proclamation Tablet."

The two had intended to have a sparring match, but just before they could begin, Lance received a sudden task, forcing him and Clair to leave.

Shortly after, Steven also got a call from work, apologized to Johan and Cynthia, and flew off on Metagross.

"Well, guess it's just us 'idle folks' left." Johan sighed.

They were certainly busy people.

Cynthia smiled faintly without replying.

She didn't get many vacations, so this quiet time was a rare treasure.

The two began heading toward the estate gates.

Steven had arranged for them to be taken care of at the manor, but both expressed their thanks and politely declined to stay.

On the way, Cynthia remarked, "Haunter seems to be having trouble mastering Shadow Ball."

Haunter, floating beside Johan, fiddled anxiously with its hands.

Cynthia continued, "Of course, it only got it yesterday."

Despite Lucario's guidance, it hadn't been long since Lucario had mastered Shadow Ball itself, and for it, Shadow Ball was more of an optional improvement, useful for covering additional types.

For Haunter, though, it was a crucial move.

Johan chuckled.

Maybe this was a blessing in disguise.

Haunter had learned new moves quickly before and had been getting a bit… big-headed about it.

"Ka-kah!!"

Suddenly—

Haunter shouted, startling both Johan and Cynthia.

They watched as its hands darkened, Shadow Claw activating.

It swiftly rubbed its hands together.

Slowly, a dark sphere formed between its palms. Although it took some time, this Shadow Ball looked far more stable than the one from earlier.

Johan's eye twitched. It was a good thing he hadn't voiced his doubts, or Haunter would've instantly proven him wrong.

But also…

What was with this technique? Was it rubbing the move together like a Rasengan?

Haunter then lobbed it into a flowerbed nearby.

Boom!

A decent-sized crater appeared.

The Pokémon in the garden scattered in alarm.

"Using Shadow Claw to cast Shadow Ball?" Cynthia remarked with intrigue.

This new technique was something she'd never seen before.

"Ka-kah!ヾ(▼▼;)"

Haunter quickly slipped its sunglasses back on, nonchalantly waving at Cynthia.

All in a day's work!

"You better remember to pay Steven for that garden repair," Johan said dryly.

"Ka-kah!! Σ(дlll)"

Haunter immediately darted toward the gates.

Johan just shook his head.

At the gate, a luxury car Steven had arranged was waiting.

As they got in, Johan cautioned Haunter, "Don't get too cocky. Sure, you learned it, but do you plan to use that technique in a battle?"

The Shadow Claw trick just doubled up on moves to create a single one, and its power wasn't all that high.

After all, Red's Eevee could topple a mountain with a single non-STAB Shadow Ball.

"It's already impressive enough," Cynthia laughed.

Behind her, Haunter beamed as it gave her a shoulder massage.

"Ka-kah ka-kah~~"

Exactly, exactly!

Johan rubbed his temples.

Great, it had "backing" now, huh?

Did it think Cynthia was sticking around?

"But, I think I know someone who might help Haunter master Shadow Ball," Cynthia said.

"Oh?"

"Ka-kah?"

Cynthia put a hand under her chin, glancing out the window as if musing to herself:

"Hm, what should we have for dinner?"

"Ka-kah!" Haunter jumped in.

Let Johan treat us!

Johan's cheek twitched.

What manners!

Just then, Vulpix raised its head and gave a soft "Oowu."

It suggested Johan cook; he made such delicious food, after all.

Alright, now Vulpix, too?

Cynthia's eyes brightened, intrigued.

"You can cook?"

Johan turned to the window.

"Not anymore."

"Hm?"

"Haunter inhaled all the gas."

"Ka-kah!! Σ(дlll)"

At a quaint little café on a quieter street in Rustboro City…

A nostalgic phonograph played soothing harmonica blues with a touch of sorrow, while wind chimes made of ceramic jingled at the door as Johan and Cynthia stepped inside.

In a cozy corner of the café sat a woman, dressed warmly with an air of elegance, savoring a slice of chocolate cake.

Cynthia led Johan toward her, her face lighting up with a warm smile.

The woman glanced up, smiling back, and the two shared a light hug.

Watching them, Johan felt a twinge of unease.

The woman removed her wide-brimmed hat and sunglasses, revealing her exquisitely made-up face, blue eyes, and ash-brown bob.

It was Diantha, the famous actress—

And Cynthia's best friend, and the future Champion of Kalos!

Diantha smiled, extending her hand toward Johan.

"So, you're Johan? Cynthia has told me a lot about you."

Momentarily taken aback, Johan shook her hand politely.

"Never thought I'd meet Ms. Diantha here in Hoenn…"

Before he could finish his sentence, Haunter's wide-eyed expression fixed on Diantha in shock.

"Ka-kah!!"

It remembered her!

But then, it felt a sudden chill from the "death glare" directed at it from behind.

# 92: Is the ‘Heir to Pokémon’ Phenomenon Happening Already?

"If you dare talk nonsense again, I'll challenge Cynthia, Lance, Steven, and even Diantha!"

That was the message in Johan's gaze.

"Ka-kah…"

A nervous drop of sweat formed on Haunter's forehead as it shuddered at the thought of such a scene.

Though it wanted to show off its "remarkable" memory by telling Diantha it had seen her entire photo collection, Johan's stern glare kept it silent.

Still, Haunter couldn't help but think: with the right "motivation," maybe it could score another move TM. It was trying to save Johan money, but it seemed the trainer just didn't understand such thriftiness.

Sigh… being a Pokémon sure is tough.

"Hm?"

Cynthia's keen instincts kicked in, sensing something amiss.

But Haunter, now vigilant under Johan's watchful eye, reacted swiftly. It quickly explained to Diantha that it had seen her films since she was a famous actress, of course!

Diantha took no offense, as she was aware of her popularity; otherwise, she wouldn't have come disguised to the café.

Still, Cynthia glanced at Johan, who had already averted his eyes and put on his best "nothing happened" face. In the end, she let it go.

Once everyone had taken their seats, Diantha asked, "How about some cake? This place is famous for it."

"Ka-kah!"

"Oowu…"

"Um…"

Haunter and Vulpix made their choices quickly, having already read the menu on the wall when they came in.

Cynthia, however, slipped into her usual indecision.

Johan sighed, then ordered, "One chocolate mousse, two double mint-vanilla parfaits, and an espresso, please."

Diantha looked surprised as Cynthia sighed in relief. From what Diantha remembered, Cynthia generally enjoyed the process of choosing, even with her choice paralysis, and rarely accepted others' suggestions.

So this is the skill of a psychologist, Diantha mused, her faith in her plan for Johan growing stronger.

As they waited for the treats, Cynthia and Diantha casually caught up, until finally, they reached the main topic.

"Here's the thing. Johan's Haunter is working on Shadow Ball but having a bit of trouble," Cynthia said, taking a small bite of cake before continuing in a crisp, clear voice, "I thought maybe your Gardevoir, since it's great with Shadow Ball, could help?"

Diantha's main partner Pokémon was indeed a Gardevoir. Though primarily a Psychic and Fairy type, Gardevoir was remarkably adept at Shadow Ball.

In the original story, when Diantha first battled Ash, it was her Gardevoir's Shadow Ball that blasted Pikachu away, and it remained a favorite move of hers.

"Of course," Diantha replied with a smile, her quick response catching Johan by surprise.

Then she tossed a Poké Ball into the air.

"Gardevoir…"

As the red light faded, Diantha's Gardevoir appeared before them.

"Ka-kah~~"

Haunter gulped down its chocolate mousse and its eyes sparkled as it eyed Gardevoir.

Heh, Gardevoir, heh...

"Haunter."

Johan called it back just in time, seizing Haunter to prevent any foolish behavior.

The ghost-type looked confused.

"Let me take a look first," Diantha said gently.

"Ka-kah?"

Here?

Since Johan didn't object, Haunter shrugged and chuckled. It raised its darkened claws and worked up a Shadow Ball.

"Using Shadow Claw to set up Shadow Ball?" Diantha murmured, admiring the unique approach.

"It only got the Shadow Ball TM last night," Cynthia added.

Diantha's eyebrow rose in surprise.

To learn Shadow Ball in under a day? While the approach was certainly unconventional, Haunter's learning aptitude was clearly impressive.

And beyond that, it also reflected Johan's skill as a trainer. Despite being unfamiliar with Johan beyond what Cynthia had told her, Diantha was intrigued.

"Go ahead, release it," she encouraged Haunter.

"Ka-kah!"

Being the "rebel without a cause," Haunter naturally didn't hesitate, tossing the Shadow Ball toward the wall.

At a glance from Diantha, Gardevoir understood.

A powerful pulse of psychic energy radiated from Gardevoir, who formed a "frame" around the Shadow Ball with its hands.

"Garde...voir."

Gardevoir's eyes sharpened in concentration, intensifying the psychic hold.

Boom!

The Shadow Ball exploded within the psychic barrier.

"Ka-kah?!"

Haunter's eyes widened, and it glanced at Johan in gratitude.

Thank goodness I didn't rush things.

"Oowu?"

Vulpix tilted its head, watching intently with an amused glimmer in its eye, as though it had found something entertaining.

"Gardevoir."

Gardevoir turned back toward Diantha, signaling it was done.

Diantha smiled. "Haunter's skills are even better than I expected."

She continued, "There's actually a small training area behind this café. Why don't Gardevoir and Haunter train there for now? And once you're back at the hotel tonight, I'll send a document with some additional suggestions. It might need certain materials, though."

Materials, huh?

In Agatha's Notes on Dread, she recommended supplements to strengthen ghost-type Pokémon, though her advice was more of a general approach than a specific moveset.

"Thank you," Johan said, nodding thoughtfully.

There goes another fortune on materials, he thought wryly.

When Haunter scampered off to the training area like a model student beside Gardevoir, Diantha turned to Johan and said, "Actually, I have something I wanted to ask—or maybe it's a bit of a favor."

Huh?

Johan blinked in surprise.

"I have a friend…" Diantha said, hesitating before continuing, "Someone who was once incredibly compassionate and kindhearted, always helping those in need."

Wait!

Johan's heart sank as she spoke. This friend sounded awfully like…

"...but recently, when I saw him, I felt as though his mind—his entire outlook—had somehow changed. It's like his ideals have become... distorted. He's even started using the term 'eternal beauty.' There's no such thing as eternal beauty, yet he's growing so certain about it… and it worries me."

That settled it.

Johan now had a strong suspicion about who Diantha's "friend" really was.

Lysandre.

The head of Team Flare in Kalos and the face behind Lysandre Labs. Team Flare, like Team Rocket, was a notorious organization with a sinister goal: to reactivate the ancient "Ultimate Weapon" from three thousand years ago to destroy all living beings!

Without waiting for her to go on, Johan quickly raised a hand, apologizing in advance.

Any more details, and he feared he'd be caught in something big.

"Uh... I'm just a Pokémon psychologist…" he said cautiously.

Diantha looked at Cynthia.

But Cynthia turned her gaze to the window.

In that moment, she wore the same expression Haunter had yesterday—except for the absent whistling sound.

Johan suddenly felt a pressure in his chest.

What was going on?

Had the "heir to Pokémon" trend really begun?
